+  /** getInt that refuses a fractional value (8.5, or "150.5" from a variable) instead of truncating
+    * it.

+  private def wholeInt(c: Config, p: String): Int =
+    val i = c.getInt(p)
+    if c.getDouble(p) != i then
+      throw new ConfigException.BadValue(c.getValue(p).origin, p, "must be a whole number")
+    i

+  test("phase 11 D11: an index entry set to null stops the load (no silently dropped index)") {
+    for k <- Seq("abstracts", "communities", "paths", "researchers", "topic-institutions") do
+      val f = file(s"scholarchain.s3-vectors.indexes.$k = null")
+      assert(errorsOf(Map(ConfigLoader.ConfigFileEnv -> f.toString)).contains(k))
+  }
+
+  test("phase 11 D12: outside local, a blank value from a file or an empty variable is refused") {
+    val fileKeys = Seq(
+      "application.region",
+      "storage.bucket",
+      "neptune.endpoint",
+      "neptune.loader-role-arn",
+      "dynamodb.table-prefix",
+      "s3-vectors.vector-bucket",
+      "s3-vectors.indexes.researchers",
+      "models.embedding-model",
+      "models.planner-model",
+      "models.synthesis-model",
+      "graph.policy-version",
+      "entity-resolution.policy-version",
+      "retrieval.fusion-policy-version",
+      "runtime.prompts-dir",
+      "services.queue.url",
+      "services.api-key-env"
+    )
+    // The variable layer wins, so drop the variable that would otherwise set the same key.
+    val pathToVar = ConfigLoader.EnvOverrides.map(_.swap)
+    for k <- fileKeys; blank <- Seq("", "  ") do
+      val f = file(s"scholarchain.$k = \"$blank\"")
+      val env = devEnv -- pathToVar.get(k) + (ConfigLoader.ConfigFileEnv -> f.toString)
+      assert(errorsOf(env).contains(k), k)
+    for v <- Seq(
+        "SCHOLARCHAIN_AWS_REGION",
+        "SCHOLARCHAIN_NEPTUNE_ENDPOINT",
+        "SCHOLARCHAIN_VECTOR_BUCKET",
+        "SCHOLARCHAIN_EMBEDDING_MODEL_ID",
+        "SCHOLARCHAIN_PLANNER_MODEL_ID",
+        "SCHOLARCHAIN_SYNTHESIS_MODEL_ID",
+        "SCHOLARCHAIN_GRAPH_POLICY_VERSION",
+        "SCHOLARCHAIN_ER_POLICY_VERSION"
+      )
+    do assert(errorsOf(devEnv + (v -> "")).contains(ConfigLoader.EnvOverrides(v)), v)
+  }
+
+  test("phase 11 D13: a fractional value for an integer key is refused, never truncated") {
+    for (v, x) <- Seq(
+        "SCHOLARCHAIN_ER_MAX_BLOCK_SIZE" -> "150.5",
+        "SCHOLARCHAIN_NEPTUNE_PORT" -> "8182.9",
+        "SCHOLARCHAIN_JOB_MAX_RECEIVE_COUNT" -> "3.7"
+      )
+    do assert(errorsOf(Map(v -> x)).contains(ConfigLoader.EnvOverrides(v)), v)
+    for h <- Seq(
+        "services.query-api.threads = 8.5",
+        "graph.oversize-team-threshold = 99.9",
+        """graph.windows = [{ name = "a", start-year = 2000.5, end-year = 2010 }]"""
+      )
+    do
+      assert(
+        errorsOf(Map(ConfigLoader.ConfigFileEnv -> file(s"scholarchain { $h }").toString))
+          .contains("whole number"),
+        h
+      )
+    assert(load("SCHOLARCHAIN_ER_MAX_BLOCK_SIZE" -> "151").entityResolution.maxBlockSize == 151)

 **Numbers (ruling 17).** Text is first stripped of every bundle text with a digit, space, or capital (names, titles, IDs, versions), so digits inside them are not numbers; window names are kept for the window rule. A year range (`2000-2014`, `2000 to 2014`) is a window mention unless both years are year facts the claim asserts (first and last collaboration years, hop years, work years), which are facts, not windows. Every other number must be bound: some values entry of the claim passed `asserted-values`, has a numeric fact name, and a cited item's stored value of that fact equals the written number. Integers compare exactly; a decimal passes when equal, or when at least 2 decimals are written and the stored value rounded half-up to them equals it (0.55 never verifies as 1 or 0.6). The sign of a stored change is not required in the text. Window keys (`window`, `*WindowStartYear`, `*WindowEndYear`) never bind a number: a window bound is not a free number. The bound fact's measure must be named: for scalar facts the measure name nearest to the number in its clause (`;`, `:`, a sentence end; parenthetical remarks that do not contain the number are skipped) must be one of the fact's names (`Verifier.Families`, for example "distinct coauthors" or "breadth" for `distinctCoauthorCount`, "publications", "papers", or "works" for `publicationCount`, "weighted degree", "intensity", or "joint publications" for `weightedDegree`, as synthesis-v2 and v3 define intensity; "average", "mean", "per paper", or "coauthors per paper" for `averageCoauthorsPerPaper`, review 3 n6); list facts (`rank.<k>.value`, `entry.<k>.count`, `CHANGE#` from, to, and delta facts) need the measure named once in the text; year facts need no name. An asserted `edgeExists` of `false` binds the number 0 as a joint publication count (the pair has no edge in that window).
 
-**Digit groups and corpus measures (verifier-v5, Phase 10 work order 9).** Before number words are converted, digit-group commas are removed (`Verifier.ungroup`): one to three digits followed by groups of exactly three digits, as in "141,367" (live B18, where the claim was removed because 141 and 367 were read as two numbers). "2001,2002", "1,23", and "12,3456" stay apart. The corpus window-summary facts have their measure names: `researchers` ("researchers", "nodes"), `components` ("components", "connected components"), `largestComponentSize` and `largestCommunitySize` (the component and community families), `largestComponentFraction` and `largestCommunityFraction` ("share", "fraction", "proportion", "percent"), `singletonResearchers` and `singletonCommunities` ("singleton(s)", "isolated"), `communities` and `communitiesAtLeastMinSize` ("community", "communities"), `communityMinSize` ("size", "members", "or more", "at least"), `communityRounds` ("rounds", "iterations"), `coauthorEdges` (the edges family), and `years` ("years"); their `from`, `to`, and `delta` forms are list facts named once in the text. +**Digit groups and corpus measures (verifier-v5, Phase 10 work order 9).** Before number words are converted, digit-group commas are removed (`Verifier.ungroup`): one to three digits followed by groups of exactly three digits, as in "141,367" (live B18, where the claim was removed because 141 and 367 were read as two numbers). +**Percentages and two measure names (verifier-v6, Phase 11 work order 2, Phase 10 ruling 25).** A number followed by "%" or "percent" binds only a share fact (`largestComponentFraction`, `largestCommunityFraction`, `topShare`, `dominantShare`, and their `from`, `to`, and `delta` forms), as the fraction it states with two more decimals than written (`Verifier.fromPercent`: "69.4" is 0.694, "68" is 0.68), so the `numberMatches` precision rule applies unchanged: 69.4% and 69% bind a stored 0.6942, 69.5% and 70% do not, and 541% never binds a count (live B13 and B24, where a correct share written as 69.4% was removed). +**Quantity words, qualifiers, and negations (verifier-v7, Phase 11 repair-01, code review 1 D1 to D3; verifier-v8, Phase 11 repair-02, D14).** A written number must state its stored value exactly; when in doubt the text fails `numbers-in-text` and the claim is removed (a summary is replaced, an uncertainty line is left out). +- quantity words: "hundred(s)", "thousand(s)", "million(s)", "billion(s)", "dozen(s)" (so "a dozen"), "scores of", "tens of", "handful", "few", "several", "many", "numerous", "countless", "multiple", "a lot of", "lots of", "plenty of", "a couple of", "a number of", "majority of", "most of", and "half of" fail when their clause also holds a measure name of any family (`Verifier.Families`), as in "hundreds of publications" or "a dozen distinct coauthors" (D1). +- qualifiers directly before a number ("more", "fewer", "less", "greater", "higher", "lower", "larger", or "smaller" + "than", optionally after "no"; "at least", "at most", "up to", "as many as", "in excess of", "upwards of", "close to", "nearly", "almost", "about", "around", "approximately", "approx", "roughly", "circa", "ca", "some", "beyond", "exceeding", "exceeds", "over", "under", "above", "below", and the signs ~ ≈ < > ≤ ≥; verifier-v8 adds "short of", "shy of", "upward of", "north of", "south of", "far from", "near", "nigh", "approaching", "nearing", "exceed" and "exceeded", "surpass" in all forms, "outnumber" in all forms, "tops", "topped", "topping" (never "top", a list size), and "beneath") or directly after it ("or more", "or fewer", "or less", "or so", "or higher", "or lower", "or greater", "or above", "or below", "or over", "or under", "or thereabouts", "+", "plus", "-odd", "and up", "and above", "and over", "and more", "at least", "at most"; verifier-v8 adds "-ish" or "ish", "and counting", and "at (a) minimum" or "at (a) maximum") fail (D2, D14). Since verifier-v8 a word before the number may be followed by "the", "a", "an", "a total of", or "a count of" ("exceeds a total of 10", "over the 10"), and the ratio exception below allows the same words. A bound fact's own direction is its stored value: "at least", "no fewer than", "no less than", "≥", or "or more" with a minimum (`communityMinSize`, `minWorks`: "37 communities have size 3 or more"), and "at most", "up to", "no more than", "≤", or "or fewer" with a maximum (`maxDepth`: "no coauthorship path of at most 4 hops"); the opposite direction still fails. "over", "under", "above", or "below" after a number or a count noun (coauthor, collaborator, publication, paper, work, article) is a ratio, not a comparison: "weighted degree is 25 over 10 publications", "1 distinct coauthor over 10 publications";
+- negations before the number in its clause ("not", "n't", "never", "neither", "nor", "false", "untrue", "incorrect", "wrong", "cannot", "no longer"; verifier-v8 adds "lack", "lacks", "lacked", "lacking", "without", "absent", "absence", "devoid", "fail(s, ed, ing) to", "hardly", "scarcely", "barely", "seldom", "rarely", "deny", "denies", "denied", "except", and "excluding") fail (D3, D14): "does not have 10 publications", "it is false that X has 10 publications", "has not 1 distinct coauthor", "lacks 10 publications", "is without 10 publications". A negation after the number ("of 3 researchers, which is not the largest connected component") or in another clause ("Breadth is not intensity: ...") does not apply to it, and "not converge" or "failed to converge" ("did not converge within its 10 rounds") states the stored `communityConverged`. Since verifier-v8 a negation inside a closed parenthetical remark before the number does not apply to it when the remark holds no digit and names a measure (`Verifier.Families`): it defines a noun, as the template's "28 singleton researchers (researchers without any coauthor edge in that window) are among the 1200 researchers". Any other parenthetical, for example "has (not) 10 publications", still negates. The measure-name phrase "on one paper" is recognised only after a team, author, coauthor, or collaborator word, so "Ada and Grace collaborated on a single paper" still states 1.
+- verifier-v7 (code review 1 D1 to D3) removes some true model claims: "at least 10 publications" or "no more than 10" against a stored 10 (true, but not the exact value), "about 10", "10 or so", and any quantity word in a clause with a measure name ("many coauthors", "several papers") are removed, a summary holding one is replaced by the kept claims, and an uncertainty line holding one is left out. Only a minimum's "at least" or "or more" and a maximum's "at most", "up to", or "or fewer" are exact. A negation anywhere before a number in its clause removes the claim even when it negates something else ("not counting reviews, X has 10 publications"), except "not converge". "over" or "under" after a count noun is read as a ratio, so "X has 1 coauthor over 10 publications" verifies, and so would "published 3 papers over 10 years" if 10 were an asserted `years` fact.
+- verifier-v8 (D14) removes more true or harmless model claims: "except" or "excluding" anywhere before a number in its clause ("Excluding reviews, X has 10 publications"), "barely 10" or "hardly 10", "near 10", and a qualifier before "the" or "a total of" ("about the 10 publications"). A negation inside a parenthetical remark that names a measure and holds no digit is skipped, so "X (whose works are not indexed elsewhere) has 10 publications" verifies; the remark itself is not checked. The comparison and negation lists are still lexical. Knowingly not covered: "past 10" ("over the past 10 years" is a time phrase), "is missing 10" (ambiguous with missing-evidence wording), "a minimum of 10" and "a maximum of 10" before the number (no bound-direction handling for them), "within 10", "toward 10", "pushing 10", "the better part of 10", "besides", "apart from", "other than", "none of", "nobody", and "nothing"; a comparison word separated from the number by other words ("exceeds the stored total of 10"); and an after-number word written after the measure ("10 publications and counting", "10 publications at minimum"). "a mere 10" states the stored value and verifies.
 - The added measure names (review 3 n6) make two families nearer: "joint publications" names both a pair's `jointPublicationCount` and a researcher's `weightedDegree`, and "coauthors per paper" names `averageCoauthorsPerPaper` as well as the coauthor families. A number equal to the other measure's stored value in a cited item then binds; the asserted key must still be in values and equal.
 - Name-like spans (review 2 n3): a span written next to a one-word bundle text ("IPDPS Workshops", where only "IPDPS" is stored) or a shortened display name ("Paolo Romano" for "Paolo Romano 0002") is now flagged and the claim removed. That errs toward removal.
 - Asserted names (ruling 29): a model claim that names a researcher whose facts it cites but does not assert in `values` (for example a publication claim citing an edge and a work, with only the work ID in values, that names the pair) is removed. A community or facet claim may name any returned member or entry once it asserts one of the item's facts. A name inside a cited title is skipped. Names are matched as whole words against the bundle's researcher names, so a name spelled differently from every bundle name is caught only by the name-like span rule, and a single-word surname is not caught.
 
-- Corpus window summaries (work order 9): the node basis is DBLP-authored researchers only, communities did not converge in any stored window (10-round cap), and the windows differ in length; each is stated as an uncertainty line, but a model may still omit it from its claims. The stored community threshold is `communityMinSize` (3); a question with another threshold ("size 5 or more") gets the count at 3 with the threshold stated, and a model claim writing 5 is removed. A model that writes a singleton count as "researchers with no coauthors" has it read as "0 coauthors" (the zero rule of section 12), so the claim is removed; "singleton researchers" or "isolated researchers" verifies. Percentages ("69.4%") are not converted, so a share written as a percentage is removed. The sign of a delta is not checked against "larger" or "smaller" in the text (as for every stored change).
-- Live verifier removals reviewed in work order 9 (ruling 19b) and kept by design: a topic name taken from a cited profile text is a name-like span that is not a whole bundle text (review 2 n3), so a DESCRIPTIVE claim listing topics from a profile is removed; a RANKING claim that writes its position in a filtered list ("ranks second") where the stored rank differs is removed (stored ranks only, review 2 n2); a PUBLICATION claim that names the researcher whose maximum-team paper it is, but asserts only the work's facts, is removed (ruling 29 N2).
+- Corpus window summaries (work order 9): the node basis is DBLP-authored researchers only, communities did not converge in any stored window (10-round cap), and the windows differ in length; each is stated as an uncertainty line, but a model may still omit it from its claims. The stored community threshold is `communityMinSize` (3); a question with another threshold ("size 5 or more") gets the count at 3 with the threshold stated, and a model claim writing 5 is removed. A model that writes a singleton count as "researchers with no coauthors" has it read as "0 coauthors" (the zero rule of section 12), so the claim is removed; "singleton researchers" or "isolated researchers" verifies. Since verifier-v6 a share written as a percentage ("69.4%" of a stored 0.6942) binds the share fact at the written precision plus 2 decimals (section 12); a percentage of a count, or one that does not round to the stored share, is removed. The sign of a delta is not checked against "larger" or "smaller" in the text (as for every stored change).
+- Live verifier removals reviewed in work order 9 (ruling 19b) and kept by design: a topic name taken from a cited profile text is a name-like span that is not a whole bundle text (review 2 n3), so a DESCRIPTIVE claim listing topics from a profile is removed; a RANKING claim that writes its position in a filtered list ("ranks second") where the stored rank differs is removed (stored ranks only, review 2 n2); a PUBLICATION claim that names the researcher whose maximum-team paper it is, but asserts only the work's facts, is removed (ruling 29 N2). Phase 11 work order 2 classified the 15 removals of the Phase 10 gate run (runtime `run-422d912ac162f340f5f31a1b63b65e19`) the same way: these three shapes (1 DESCRIPTIVE on B18, 9 RANKING on B26, 1 PUBLICATION on B05) and B23's IDENTITY claim, which stated the name's `totalWorks` as the researcher's own count, are removals by design; the B13, B24 (percentages) and B17 ("collaboration communities") removals were verifier defects, fixed in verifier-v6.
 - Planner-v2 (ruling 30) is tested only on recorded-shape fixtures. The step-1 audits keep the failed rules but not the model's window fields, so the rule-7 fixture (NAMED `2015-2026` with its years filled in) is a reconstruction. +  test("Phase 11 work order 2, live B13 and B24 shape: a share written as a percentage") {
+    withWorld(summaries = true) { w =>
+      val r = ok(w.ask(b24))
+      val c = Verifier.Context(r.bundle.get, r.answer.resolutions, config)
+      val e = r.bundle.get.graphEvidence
+        .find(x => x.evidenceType == EvidenceType.COMPONENT_RECORD)
+        .get
+      assert(e.facts("window") == "2015-2026" && e.facts("largestComponentFraction") == "0.67625")
+      val values = Map(
+        "largestComponentSize" -> "541",
+        "researchers" -> "800",
+        "largestComponentFraction" -> "0.6763"
+      )
+      def status(text: String, v: Map[String, String] = values) =
+        Verifier
+          .check(
+            ClaimDraft(
+              "claim-1",
+              ClaimKind.COMPONENT,
+              text,
+              Vector(e.evidenceId),
+              Vector.empty,
+              Some("2015-2026"),
+              v
+            ),
+            c
+          )
+          .status
+      def claim(share: String) =
+        s"In 2015-2026, the largest connected component has 541 researchers, $share of the 800 researchers."
+      // The stored fraction 0.67625 as a percentage, to the precision written.
+      for share <- Vector("67.6%", "68%", "67.63 percent", "a 67.6% share") do
+        assert(status(claim(share)) == VerificationStatus.VERIFIED, share)
+      // Another value, a misplaced point, a count written as a percentage, or a share the claim
+      // does not assert, is removed.
+      for share <- Vector("67.7%", "67%", "6.8%", "0.68%", "541%") do
+        assert(status(claim(share)) == VerificationStatus.UNSUPPORTED, share)
+      assert(
+        status(claim("67.6%"), values - "largestComponentFraction") ==
+          VerificationStatus.UNSUPPORTED
+      )
+    }
+  }

+        val r = ok(w.ask(q))

+            "are among the 1200 researchers of the coauthorship graph."

+      )

+      )

+          VerificationStatus.VERIFIED
+      )
+      assert(
+        rounds("Community detection in 2000-2014 lacked its 10 rounds.") ==
+          VerificationStatus.UNSUPPORTED
+      )
+    }

+  test("Phase 11 work order 2, live B23 shape: totalWorks is named as the works of the name") {
+    context(pairQ) { (c0, _) =>
+      // The failure shape: the identity item of a name shared by several researchers, where the
+      // resolved researcher has fewer works than all researchers with the name together.
+      val b = c0.bundle
+      val id = b.graphEvidence
+        .find(e =>
+          e.evidenceType == EvidenceType.IDENTITY_RESOLUTION &&
+            e.facts.get("displayName").contains("Ada Lovelace")
+        )
+        .get
+      val c = c0.copy(bundle =
+        b.copy(graphEvidence =
+          b.graphEvidence.map(e =>
+            if e.evidenceId == id.evidenceId then
+              e.copy(facts = e.facts + ("works" -> "40") + ("totalWorks" -> "52"))
+            else e
+          )
+        )
+      )
+      def d(text: String, values: Map[String, String]) =
+        draft(ClaimKind.IDENTITY, text, Vector(id.evidenceId), values, None, id.subjectIds)
+      val both = Map("works" -> "40", "totalWorks" -> "52")
+      // The template wording verifies.
+      val template =
+        d("Ada Lovelace: 40 of 52 works among researchers with this display name.", both)
+      assert(status(template, c) == VERIFIED, Verifier.check(template, c).checks)
+      // The total of every researcher with the name is never the researcher's own count.
+      for text <- Vector(
+          "Ada Lovelace is a researcher with 52 total works.",
+          "Ada Lovelace has 52 works.",
+          "Ada Lovelace has 52 publications."
+        )
+      do
+        val k = Verifier.check(d(text, Map("totalWorks" -> "52")), c)
+        assert(k.status == UNSUPPORTED, text)
+        assert(k.checks.exists(x => x.rule == "numbers-in-text" && x.detail.contains("totalWorks")))
+    }
+  }

+  test("N5 (Phase 10 review 2): fixed notes are read with number words before year ranges") {
+    context(pairQ) { (c, r) =>
+      val good = ClaimDraft(
+        "claim-1",
+        ClaimKind.COAUTHORSHIP,

+        Vector(edgeEv),
+        Vector(ada, grace),
+        Some("all-years"),
+        Map("jointPublicationCount" -> "2")
+      )
+      // "first" before a year range is a time marker, not 1st: the note frees no number 1.
+      val note = "Requested 2005-2010; the first stored window 2000-2014 contains it."
+      val v = Verifier.verify(
+        SynthesisOutput(
+          RuntimeSchemas.SynthesisOutput,
+          good.text,
+          Vector(good),
+          Vector("Requested 2005-2010 maps to 2000-2014.", "There may be 1 more paper.")
+        ),
+        c.copy(notes = Vector(note)),
+        "qry-x",
+        r.answer.response.provenance
+      )
+      assert(v.uncertainty.head == "Requested 2005-2010 maps to 2000-2014.", v.uncertainty)
+      assert(v.dropped.map(_.text) == Vector("There may be 1 more paper."), v.dropped)
+    }
+  }

+  private def adaClaim(text: String, values: Map[String, String]) =
+    draft(ClaimKind.METRIC_VALUE, text, Vector(adaMetric), values)
+  private val pubs = Map("publicationCount" -> "10")
+  private val coauthors = Map("distinctCoauthorCount" -> "1")
+  private def removed(c: Verifier.Context, cases: Vector[(String, Map[String, String])]) =
+    cases.foreach { (t, v) =>
+      val k = Verifier.check(adaClaim(t, v), c)
+      assert(k.status == UNSUPPORTED, t)
+      assert(k.checks.exists(x => x.rule == "numbers-in-text" && x.outcome == CheckOutcome.FAIL), t)
+    }

+  test("repair-01 D1: a quantity in words (hundreds of, dozens of, a dozen) is not verified") {
+    context(pairQ) { (c, _) =>
+      assert(
+        status(adaClaim("In all-years, Ada Lovelace has 10 publications.", pubs), c) == VERIFIED
+      )
+      removed(
+        c,
+        Vector(
+          "In all-years, Ada Lovelace has hundreds of publications." -> pubs,
+          "In all-years, Ada Lovelace has dozens of publications." -> pubs,
+          "In all-years, Ada Lovelace has scores of publications." -> pubs,
+          "In all-years, Ada Lovelace has a dozen distinct coauthors." -> coauthors,
+          "In all-years, Ada Lovelace has several publications." -> pubs,
+          "In all-years, Ada Lovelace has ten thousand publications." -> pubs
+        )
+      )
+    }
+  }

+  test("repair-01 D2: a qualified number (more than 10, about 10, 10 or more) is not verified") {
+    context(pairQ) { (c, _) =>
+      removed(
+        c,
+        Vector(
+          "In all-years, Ada Lovelace has more than 10 publications.",
+          "In all-years, Ada Lovelace has fewer than 10 publications.",
+          "In all-years, Ada Lovelace has over 10 publications.",
+          "In all-years, Ada Lovelace has under 10 publications.",
+          "In all-years, Ada Lovelace has at least 10 publications.",
+          "In all-years, Ada Lovelace has at most 10 publications.",
+          "In all-years, Ada Lovelace has nearly 10 publications.",
+          "In all-years, Ada Lovelace has about ten publications.",
+          "In all-years, Ada Lovelace has approximately 10 publications.",
+          "In all-years, Ada Lovelace has 10 or more publications.",
+          "In all-years, Ada Lovelace has 10+ publications."
+        ).map(_ -> pubs)
+      )
+      // "1 coauthor over 10 publications" is a ratio, not "more than 10".
+      val both = pubs ++ coauthors
+      assert(
+        status(
+          adaClaim(
+            "In all-years, Ada Lovelace has 1 distinct coauthor over 10 publications.",
+            both
+          ),
+          c
+        ) ==
+          VERIFIED
+      )
+    }
+  }

+  test("repair-01 controls: bound qualifiers and the template's negations still verify") {
+    context(pathQ) { (c0, _) =>
+      // A path search cut at its depth: "at most 4 hops" states the stored maxDepth exactly.
+      val b = c0.bundle
+      val path = b.graphEvidence.find(_.evidenceType == EvidenceType.GRAPH_PATH).get
+      val cut = path.copy(facts = path.facts + ("status" -> "BEYOND_DEPTH") + ("maxDepth" -> "4"))
+      val c = c0.copy(bundle =
+        b.copy(graphEvidence = b.graphEvidence.map(e => if e == path then cut else e))
+      )
+      val v = Map("status" -> "BEYOND_DEPTH", "maxDepth" -> "4")
+      def d(text: String) =
+        draft(ClaimKind.PATH, text, Vector(cut.evidenceId), v, subjects = path.subjectIds)
+      val names = "connects Ada Lovelace and Edsger Dijkstra in all-years."
+      val template = d(s"No coauthorship path of at most 4 hops $names")
+      assert(status(template, c) == VERIFIED, Verifier.check(template, c).checks)
+      assert(status(d(s"No coauthorship path of up to 4 hops $names"), c) == VERIFIED)
+      // The bound's own direction only: "at least" of a maximum is not its stored value.
+      assert(status(d(s"No coauthorship path of at least 4 hops $names"), c) == UNSUPPORTED)
+      assert(status(d(s"No coauthorship path of more than 4 hops $names"), c) == UNSUPPORTED)
+    }
+    // The template's "which is not the largest connected component" after the size.
+    context("Which connected component is Ada Lovelace in?") { (c0, _) =>
+      val b = c0.bundle
+      val comp = b.graphEvidence.find(_.evidenceType == EvidenceType.COMPONENT_RECORD).get
+      val notLargest = comp.copy(facts = comp.facts + ("inLargestComponent" -> "false"))
+      val c = c0.copy(bundle =
+        b.copy(graphEvidence = b.graphEvidence.map(e => if e == comp then notLargest else e))
+      )
+      val v = Map(
+        "componentId" -> comp.facts("componentId"),
+        "componentSize" -> comp.facts("componentSize"),
+        "inLargestComponent" -> "false"
+      )
+      val text = s"In all-years, Ada Lovelace is in connected component ${v("componentId")} of " +
+        s"${v("componentSize")} researchers, which is not the largest connected component of that window."
+      val k = Verifier.check(
+        draft(ClaimKind.COMPONENT, text, Vector(notLargest.evidenceId), v),
+        c
+      )
+      assert(k.status == VERIFIED, k.checks)
+    }
+    // The fake template's own wordings: "is 25 over 10 publications", "Breadth is not intensity:",
+    // "Bridge importance is not breadth:"; every material claim verifies.
+    for (q, wording) <- Vector(
+        "How many distinct coauthors does Grace Hopper have?" -> "Breadth is not intensity:",
+        "Is Grace Hopper a bridge between collaboration communities?" -> "Bridge importance is not"
+      )
+    do
+      withWorld() { w =>
+        val a = ok(w.ask(q)).answer
+        val texts = a.response.claims.map(c => (c.verificationStatus, c.text))
+        assert(texts.exists((s, t) => s == VERIFIED && t.contains(wording)), (q, texts))
+        assert(a.verification.counts.unsupported == 0, (q, a.verification))
+        assert(a.verification.counts.conflicting == 0, (q, a.verification))
+      }
+  }

+  test("repair-01 D4: a failed vector query degrades semantic context; graph facts still answer") {
+    // Every query throws a transient store error (after the storage retry policy), or is refused.
+    val failures: Vector[(String, () => Either[String, Vector[scholarchain.storage.VectorHit]])] =
+      Vector(
+        "thrown" -> (() =>
+          throw scholarchain.storage.TransientStoreException("ThrottlingException (fixture)")
+        ),
+        "refused" -> (() => Left("ValidationException (fixture)"))
+      )
+    for (shape, failure) <- failures do
+      withWorld() { w =>
+        var calls = 0
+        val vectors = new scholarchain.storage.VectorReadPort:
+          def get(index: String, keys: Seq[String]) = w.ports.vectors.get(index, keys)
+          def query(
+              index: String,
+              vector: Array[Float],
+              topK: Int,
+              filter: Option[scholarchain.storage.VectorFilter]
+          ) =
+            calls += 1
+            failure()
+        val r = Workflow.answer(
+          QueryRequest(
+            RuntimeSchemas.QueryRequest,
+            "",
+            Some("session-1"),
+            pairQ,
+            None,
+            None,
+            None,
+            None
+          ),
+          w.ports.copy(vectors = vectors),
+          w.setup
+        )
+        val res = r.fold(f => fail(s"$shape: $f"), identity)
+        val a = res.answer
+        assert(calls >= 1, shape)
+        assert(a.abstention.isEmpty, (shape, a.abstention))
+        assert(res.bundle.get.semanticEvidence.isEmpty, shape)
+        assert(
+          res.bundle.get.missing.exists(m =>
+            m.reason == MissingReason.NO_SEMANTIC_MATCH && m.detail.contains("query failed")
+          ),
+          (shape, res.bundle.get.missing)
+        )
+        val joint = a.response.claims.find(_.text.contains("2 joint publications")).get
+        assert(joint.verificationStatus == VerificationStatus.VERIFIED, shape)
+        assert(
+          res.audit.stages.exists(s =>
+            s.stage == Stage.SEMANTIC_CONTEXT && s.outcome == StageOutcome.OK
+          ),
+          shape
+        )
+      }
+  }

 | Service | HTTP threads | Store pool (store calls and readiness probes) | Workflow pool (no queue) |
 |---|---|---|---|
 | query-api | `services.query-api.threads` = T (8) | T threads, queue `services.max-queued-requests` (64) | `max(1, T / 2)` slots (4): the sync workflow (`RuntimeApi.answer`) |
-| graph-evidence-api | `services.graph-evidence-api.threads` = G (16) | G threads, queue 64 | 1 slot, never used: it runs no workflow, and no thread is started for it |
+| graph-evidence-api | `services.graph-evidence-api.threads` = G (16) | G threads, queue 64 | `max(1, G / 2)` slots (8), never used: it runs no workflow, and a slot's thread starts only on first use, so none starts |
 | worker | `services.worker.threads` = W (2) | `services.worker.concurrency` + W = C + W threads (3), queue 64 | C slots (1): the job workflow |
 
 Which calls run on which pool:
@@ -49,19 +49,21 @@ This replaces the work order 5 risk text, "sustained slow model calls can theref
 
 - **Every workflow slot of query-api is held.** The slots may be held by sync workflows still running, or by workflows past their 504 hard timeout. Those run on to their cooperative deadline, which a model call can overrun by up to `runtime.llm.timeout-ms`.
   - A new sync `POST /v1/query` answers 503 OVERLOADED (retryable) at once and touches no breaker.
+  - **Idempotency-Key on that 503.** The key is claimed before the workflow is submitted, and the claim is not released when the workflow cannot start (the release is deferred by Phase 10 ruling 18). The key therefore stays claimed until its TTL (`services.ttl.idempotency-hours`, 24). A retry with the same key and body answers 409 IDEMPOTENCY_CONFLICT ("no stored answer"), not a new attempt. To retry after a 503, send a new Idempotency-Key.
   - Async POSTs, `GET /v1/jobs/{jobId}`, `GET /v1/jobs/{jobId}/result`, `GET /v1/explain/{queryId}`, idempotent replays, and `/readyz` keep their own threads. They answer within their store timeouts.
   - `/readyz` stays ready, and no breaker opens.
   - Tested: `SaturationSpec` "query-api: with every workflow slot held ...".
 - **Every workflow slot of the worker is held** (a job past its backstop, `runtime.question.deadline-ms` + `runtime.llm.timeout-ms`).
   - The poll loop does not receive.
-  - A message already received is not claimed. Its job stays QUEUED, and the message comes back after the visibility timeout.
+  - A message already received is not claimed. Its job stays QUEUED, and the worker keeps the message (it is neither deleted nor made visible early). It comes back after the full visibility timeout, and that receive counts toward `services.queue.max-receive-count`.
+  - **Repeated misses.** A QUEUED job has no last-delivery rule (only a RUNNING job does). If its message is received without a free slot `max-receive-count` times (3), the queue's redrive policy moves the message to the dead-letter queue, and the job stays QUEUED until its TTL (`services.ttl.job-hours`, 168); `GET /v1/jobs/{jobId}` keeps answering QUEUED. With `services.worker.concurrency` = 1 this needs the brief `getActiveCount` window after a job ends; with concurrency > 1, two poll loops racing for the last slot.
   - Readiness and job-state writes keep their threads, and no breaker opens.
   - Tested: `SaturationSpec` "worker: ...".

   - A store call still waiting when its timeout passes answers 503 OVERLOADED, and no breaker counts it.
   - A readiness probe that cannot run makes `/readyz` report NOT_READY for that downstream while the pool is full. No breaker opens, so readiness returns as soon as threads free.

+
+## 6. Request read timeout and unread bodies (Phase 11 D5)
+
+The JDK HTTP server reads a request's line, headers, and body on an HTTP thread. When a route had not read the whole body, the server drained it (up to 64 KiB) as the exchange ended, after the response was written, and it waited for that body with no time limit. A client that declared a body and never sent it therefore held an HTTP thread and its connection indefinitely, with or without the API key (code review 1, D5). Two limits now apply:
+
+| Limit | Value | Configured where |
+|---|---|---|
+| Request read timeout: the JDK property `sun.net.httpserver.maxReqTime` | 30 s | the services-local constant `HttpService.RequestReadTimeoutSeconds`, set by `HttpService.start` before the JVM creates its first server (`HttpService.limitRequestReads`). A JVM option `-Dsun.net.httpserver.maxReqTime=<seconds>` set at start wins. |
+| Unread-body drain bound | 1000 ms | the services-local constant `HttpService.UnreadBodyDrainMs` |
+
+Neither is a `services.*` key; adding one is a `modules/core` change (domain-engineer).
+
+- **Request read timeout.** A request whose line, headers, and body have not all arrived 30 s after its first byte loses its connection; the client gets no response. This covers a slow or missing body that a route is reading, and headers that never end. A body read cut this way is logged 400 INVALID_FIELD ("the body was not received in full"), not 500. A response that is slow to compute is not limited: the request is complete once its body has been read.
+  - The 30 s also counts the time a request waits in the HTTP queue (`services.max-queued-requests`) for a thread, because the JDK reads the request on that thread. A request still queued after 30 s is dropped the same way.
+  - The property is JVM-wide and read once, when the JVM creates its first JDK HTTP server. Every JDK server in this repository is an `HttpService`, so it always applies.
+- **Unread bodies.** A response to a request whose body the route refused or never read (401, 400 before the body is read, 400 REQUEST_TOO_LARGE, 503 OVERLOADED when shed) is written at once with `Connection: close`. The server then drains what arrives of that body (at most 64 KiB) for at most 1000 ms and closes the connection. A client still sending a small body therefore reads its response rather than a connection reset, and a body that never comes holds the thread for at most 1000 ms: the HTTP thread is interrupted, which closes that connection. When a request is shed on the dispatcher thread (the shed queue is full too), the same 1000 ms bound applies to the dispatcher.
+- Tested: `RequestReadSpec` (raw sockets on 127.0.0.1:0):
+  - the D5 inputs (999999999 declared bytes with and without the key, and `GET /healthz` with `Content-Length: 100` and no body) are answered 400, 401, and 200 and closed within the 5 s guard, one after another on one HTTP thread;
+  - a 9-of-100-byte body and an unfinished header are closed at the timeout, the body read is logged 400 INVALID_FIELD, and the threads serve again;
+  - a keep-alive request whose full body was refused gets `Connection: close`.

+  private val alarms = ScheduledThreadPoolExecutor(
+    1,
+    r =>
+      val t = Thread(r, s"$name-alarm")
+      t.setDaemon(true)
+      t
+  )
+  alarms.setRemoveOnCancelPolicy(true)
+
+  /** Runs body; if it still runs after ms, interrupts this thread, which closes the connection a
+    * blocked socket read waits on. The interrupt never outlives body.
+    */
+  private def interruptAfter(ms: Long)(body: => Unit): Unit =
+    val self = Thread.currentThread()
+    val lock = Object()
+    var running = true
+    var fired = false
+    val alarm = alarms.schedule(
+      (() => lock.synchronized { if running then { fired = true; self.interrupt() } }): Runnable,
+      ms,
+      TimeUnit.MILLISECONDS
+    )
+    try body
+    finally
+      val clear = lock.synchronized { running = false; fired }
+      alarm.cancel(false)
+      if clear then Thread.interrupted()
 
   @volatile private var server: Option[HttpServer] = None

 /** Repair-01 F1: long workflows hold only the workflow slots. Store calls and readiness probes keep
   * their own threads, answer within their timeouts, and open no breaker against a healthy store; a
   * workflow that cannot start answers 503 OVERLOADED.

+  * Phase 11 work order 1: no assertion measures a duration. Held workflows signal when they have
+  * started and stay held until the test releases them, and the hard timeouts under test always fire

 - **Open.** One `get` of the key, then `parse`; any violation refuses the whole object (nothing half-opened). The rows are then answered from memory. Fake: `FileObjectStore(root).reader`. Live: `S3ObjectStore(...).reader` (one GetObject, the existing adapter, no new live code). Both stores are write-once, so the copy never goes stale.
 - **Read.** `windowSummary(window)` returns the row or None for a name with no row. The benchmark alias `all` is not a stored name: the caller maps it to the first configured window (`all-years`), as BenchmarkPathJob does.
-- **Validation** (`WindowSummaries.parse`, codes): `BAD_KEY` (key rules, or not under `curated/window-summary/`); `MALFORMED` (not JSON, a field missing or extra, a wrong type), so long text or any other extra field is refused; `BAD_ROW` (schema version; a configured window with its years; `years`; counts non-negative and consistent; when researchers > 0, `comp-`/`comm-` IDs, largest sizes from 1 to researchers, and each fraction equal to size / researchers within 1e-12; when researchers = 0, no IDs, no fractions, sizes 0; `communityMinSize` at least 1; non-empty `nodeBasis`; `sourceRuns` run IDs and match-policy version; provenance `provenance-v1`, DERIVED, `sourceRecordId` = window, run ID, snapshot, a policy version, null model version); `DUPLICATE_WINDOW`; `MISSING_WINDOW` (every configured window must be present); `PROVENANCE` (rows from more than one run, or a key snapshot that differs from the rows'); `OBJECT_MISSING`.

 - **Conversion** (`WindowSummaries.fromSparkManifest`). Storage has no Parquet reader, so the loader reads the Spark run's `_manifest.json`. Its `counts.windowSummary` holds every column except `nodeBasis`, `sourceRuns`, and `provenance`. Those come from `settings.nodeBasis` and `sourceRuns`, and provenance is rebuilt as the job builds it (spark `Canonical.provenance`: `provenance-v1`, DERIVED, the window, the run's snapshot and run ID, `settings.graphPolicy`, null model). The manifest must have schema `spark-run-manifest-v1`, stage `spark-corpus-window-summary`, the `corpus/window-summary` table with exactly `SparkSchema`, `spark-corpus-window-summary-v1`, and a row count equal to the rows, and no integrity violation (`INTEGRITY_VIOLATIONS`); otherwise `BAD_MANIFEST`. A Spark column change therefore stops the loader. This is a shortcut until spark-engineer writes the store object directly.
 - **Idempotency.** The same Spark run gives the same load run ID and the same bytes, so a reload is `Unchanged`; different bytes under the key are a `Conflict`, are not written, and exit 1.

   )
   private val ComponentId = "comp-[0-9a-f]{32}".r
 
+  /** Whether c groups, s of them of one member and the largest of l members, can partition r
+    * researchers (components and communities both do, singletons included). BigInt, so a tampered
+    * value cannot overflow into a pass.
+    */
+  private def partitionOk(r: BigInt, c: BigInt, l: BigInt, s: BigInt): Boolean =
+    val big = c - s // groups of two or more; the largest is one of them
+    if r == 0 then c == 0 && l == 0 && s == 0
+    else if l == 1 then s == c && c == r
+    else l >= 2 && big >= 1 && s + l + 2 * (big - 1) <= r && r - s <= l * big
+
+  /** Count identities of a real summary (Phase 11 D10): each partition is possible, the edges fit
+    * the components, and communitiesAtLeastMinSize fits the community sizes. Pairwise bounds alone
+    * accept counts no graph can have.
+    */
+  private def identityProblems(s: WindowSummary): List[String] =
+    val List(r, e, comps, lc, singles, c, l, sc, a, m) = List(
+      s.researchers,
+      s.coauthorEdges,
+      s.components,
+      s.largestComponentSize,
+      s.singletonResearchers,
+      s.communities,
+      s.largestCommunitySize,
+      s.singletonCommunities,
+      s.communitiesAtLeastMinSize,
+      s.communityMinSize.toLong
+    ).map(BigInt(_)): @unchecked
+    // a spanning forest needs researchers - components edges; a node has at most largest - 1
+    val edgesOk = r - comps <= e && 2 * e <= (r - singles) * (lc - 1).max(0)
+    // communities of at least m: all of them when m = 1, exactly the non-singletons when m = 2
+    val atMost = if m <= 1 then c else c - sc
+    val atLeastOk =
+      if l < m then a == 0
+      else a >= 1 && a <= atMost && (m > 2 || a == atMost) && (a - 1) * m + l + (c - a) <= r
+    List(
+      Option.unless(partitionOk(r, comps, lc, singles))(
+        "components cannot partition the researchers"
+      ),
+      Option.unless(partitionOk(r, c, l, sc))("communities cannot partition the researchers"),
+      Option.unless(edgesOk)("coauthorEdges do not fit the components"),
+      Option.unless(atLeastOk)("communitiesAtLeastMinSize does not fit the community sizes")
+    ).flatten
+
   /** Rule violations of one row; empty means valid. */
   def problems(s: WindowSummary, windows: Map[String, TimeWindow]): List[String] =
     def check(ok: Boolean, msg: => String) = if ok then Nil else List(msg)

+        largestComponentFraction = Some(lc.toDouble / 1200),
+        largestCommunityFraction = Some(lcm.toDouble / 1200)
+      )
+    val control = sized(900, 60)
+    assert(rowCodes(control).isEmpty)
+    // the fixture row is a forest (edges = researchers - components); a clique of 5 plus 5
+    // singletons (edges at the degree bound) is real too
+    val g = good.head
+    assert(rowCodes(g).isEmpty && g.coauthorEdges == g.researchers - g.components)
+    assert(
+      rowCodes(
+        g.copy(
+          coauthorEdges = 10,
+          sumJointPublicationCount = 10,
+          components = 6,
+          singletonResearchers = 5
+        )
+      ).isEmpty
+    )
+    val impossible = Vector(
+      // the three WO3 failure inputs
+      "largest component 900 with 1,000 components" -> control.copy(components = 1000),
+      "1,100 communities with a largest of 60" -> control.copy(communities = 1100),
+      "64 communities of at least 3 when the largest has 2" -> sized(900, 2),
+      // the work order's identities one by one, and the edge bounds
+      "largest component above researchers - (components - 1)" ->
+        control.copy(components = 302, singletonResearchers = 0),
+      "largest community above researchers - (communities - 1)" ->
+        control.copy(communities = 1142, singletonCommunities = 1000),
+      // each of these community partitions is possible; only communitiesAtLeastMinSize is not
+      "communitiesAtLeastMinSize x communityMinSize above researchers" -> sized(900, 3)
+        .copy(communities = 500, singletonCommunities = 0, communitiesAtLeastMinSize = 401),
+      "communitiesAtLeastMinSize > 0 when the largest community is below the minimum" ->
+        sized(900, 2).copy(communities = 650, singletonCommunities = 100),
+      "more communities of at least 3 than communities of two or more" ->
+        control.copy(communitiesAtLeastMinSize = 100),
+      "fewer edges than a spanning forest" -> control.copy(coauthorEdges = 1154),
+      "more edges than the degree bound allows" ->
+        g.copy(coauthorEdges = 17, sumJointPublicationCount = 17),
+      "overflowing counts" -> control.copy(
+        coauthorEdges = Long.MaxValue,
+        sumJointPublicationCount = Long.MaxValue
+      )
+    )
+    impossible.foreach { (name, s) =>
+      val vs = rowCodes(s)
+      assert(vs.nonEmpty && vs.forall(_.code == "BAD_ROW"), s"$name: $vs")
+    }
+  }
+

===== UNTRACKED FILE: modules/services/src/test/scala/scholarchain/services/RequestReadSpec.scala (130 lines) =====
     1	package scholarchain.services
     2	
     3	import org.scalatest.funsuite.AnyFunSuite
     4	
     5	import java.net.{InetSocketAddress, Socket, SocketException, SocketTimeoutException}
     6	import java.nio.charset.StandardCharsets.{ISO_8859_1, UTF_8}
     7	import java.util.concurrent.{LinkedBlockingQueue, TimeUnit}
     8	
     9	/** Phase 11 D5 over raw loopback sockets: a declared body that never arrives, or arrives too
    10	  * slowly, cannot hold an HTTP thread, with or without the API key. A body a route refused or never
    11	  * read may still arrive for HttpService.UnreadBodyDrainMs after the response, and every request
    12	  * must arrive within HttpService.RequestReadTimeoutSeconds (SERVICES.md section 6).
    13	  */
    14	class RequestReadSpec extends AnyFunSuite:
    15	  import ServiceFixtures.*
    16	
    17	  private def open(port: Int, request: String): Socket =
    18	    val s = Socket()
    19	    s.connect(InetSocketAddress("127.0.0.1", port), 10000)
    20	    s.getOutputStream.write(request.getBytes(ISO_8859_1))
    21	    s.getOutputStream.flush()
    22	    s
    23	
    24	  /** What the server sent before it closed the connection; None when guardSeconds passed first. The
    25	    * guard is a hang detector: a healthy server closes long before it.
    26	    */
    27	  private def untilClosed(s: Socket, guardSeconds: Int): Option[String] =
    28	    try
    29	      s.setSoTimeout(guardSeconds * 1000)
    30	      Some(String(s.getInputStream.readAllBytes(), UTF_8))
    31	    catch
    32	      case _: SocketTimeoutException => None
    33	      case _: SocketException        => Some("") // a reset also closes the connection
    34	    finally s.close()
    35	
    36	  private def head(method: String, path: String, length: Long, key: Boolean) =
    37	    s"$method $path HTTP/1.1\r\nHost: 127.0.0.1\r\n" +
    38	      (if key then s"X-Api-Key: $ApiKey\r\n" else "") +
    39	      s"Content-Type: application/json\r\nContent-Length: $length\r\nConnection: close\r\n\r\n"
    40	
    41	  private def status(raw: String): Int =
    42	    raw.linesIterator.nextOption.flatMap(_.split(' ').lift(1)).flatMap(_.toIntOption).getOrElse(-1)
    43	
    44	  private def error(raw: String): Option[String] =
    45	    val body = raw.indexOf("\r\n\r\n") match
    46	      case -1 => ""
    47	      case i  => raw.substring(i + 4)
    48	    Option.when(body.nonEmpty)(ujson.read(body)).flatMap(_.obj.get("error")).flatMap(_.strOpt)
    49	
    50	  test("D5: a refused or unread body that never arrives does not hold the exchange or the thread") {
    51	    // One HTTP thread: an exchange left open would also hold every later case.
    52	    withWorld("services.query-api.threads = 1") { w =>
    53	      val svc = Apps.queryApi(w.cfg, w.backend, w.auth, log)
    54	      val port = svc.start("127.0.0.1", 0)
    55	      try
    56	        Vector(
    57	          (
    58	            "over the cap, with the key",
    59	            head("POST", "/v1/query", 999999999L, key = true) + queryJson(),
    60	            400,
    61	            Some("REQUEST_TOO_LARGE")
    62	          ),
    63	          (
    64	            "over the cap, no key",
    65	            head("POST", "/v1/query", 999999999L, key = false) + queryJson(),
    66	            401,
    67	            Some("UNAUTHORIZED")
    68	          ),
    69	          ("unread body on /healthz, no key", head("GET", "/healthz", 100L, key = false), 200, None)
    70	        ).foreach { (name, request, expected, code) =>
    71	          // A healthy exchange ends UnreadBodyDrainMs (1 s) after its response. The 5 s guard is far
    72	          // below the 30 s read timeout, the only other end of a body that never comes.
    73	          val raw = untilClosed(open(port, request), 5)
    74	          assert(raw.exists(r => status(r) == expected && error(r) == code), s"$name: $raw")
    75	        }
    76	      finally svc.stop()
    77	    }
    78	  }
    79	
    80	  test("D5: a body or header slower than the read timeout loses its connection; threads freed") {
    81	    withWorld("services.query-api.threads = 2") { w =>
    82	      val lines = LinkedBlockingQueue[String]()
    83	      val svc = Apps.queryApi(w.cfg, w.backend, w.auth, JsonLog(l => lines.add(l)))
    84	      val port = svc.start("127.0.0.1", 0)
    85	      try
    86	        // With the key and within the cap, so the route reads the body: 9 of 100 bytes come.
    87	        val slowBody = open(
    88	          port,
    89	          head("POST", "/v1/query", 100L, key = true)
    90	            .replace("Host:", "X-Request-Id: d5-slow-body\r\nHost:") + "{\"questio"
    91	        )
    92	        // No key, and the headers never end.
    93	        val slowHead = open(port, "GET /healthz HTTP/1.1\r\nHost: 127.0.0.1\r\n")
    94	        val guard = HttpService.RequestReadTimeoutSeconds * 3
    95	        assert(untilClosed(slowBody, guard).isDefined, "the slow body kept its connection")
    96	        assert(untilClosed(slowHead, guard).isDefined, "the slow header kept its connection")
    97	        // The cut read is the client's failure: 400 INVALID_FIELD in the log, never a 500.
    98	        val logged = Iterator
    99	          .continually(lines.poll(guard, TimeUnit.SECONDS))
   100	          .takeWhile(_ != null)
   101	          .map(ujson.read(_))
   102	          .find(_.obj.get("requestId").flatMap(_.strOpt).contains("d5-slow-body"))
   103	        assert(
   104	          logged.exists(l => l("status").num == 400 && l("error").strOpt.contains("INVALID_FIELD")),
   105	          logged
   106	        )
   107	        assert(Client(port).get("/healthz", Seq()).status == 200)
   108	      finally svc.stop()
   109	    }
   110	  }
   111	
   112	  test("D5: a response to a body left unread says Connection: close, so no client reuses it") {
   113	    withWorld() { w =>
   114	      val svc = Apps.queryApi(w.cfg, w.backend, w.auth, log)
   115	      val port = svc.start("127.0.0.1", 0)
   116	      try
   117	        // Keep-alive (no Connection header), the whole body sent at once, refused for the key.
   118	        val body = queryJson()
   119	        val request = "POST /v1/query HTTP/1.1\r\nHost: 127.0.0.1\r\n" +
   120	          s"Content-Type: application/json\r\nContent-Length: ${body.getBytes(UTF_8).length}\r\n" +
   121	          s"\r\n$body"
   122	        val raw = untilClosed(open(port, request), 5)
   123	        val headers = raw.toSeq.flatMap(_.linesIterator.takeWhile(_.nonEmpty))
   124	        assert(
   125	          raw.exists(status(_) == 401) && headers.exists(_.equalsIgnoreCase("Connection: close")),
   126	          raw
   127	        )
   128	      finally svc.stop()
   129	    }
   130	  }

===== UNTRACKED FILE: tests/TESTS.md (140 lines) =====
     1	# ScholarChain cross-module and adversarial tests (`tests/`)
     2	
     3	Owner: test-engineer. Phase 11 work order 3 (the spec Phase 11 test work order) created this tree, work order 4 (the rerun after repair-01) extended it, and work order 5 (the rerun after repair-02) removed the D14 wrapper and added the verifier-v8 tests. It holds the cross-module, integration, end-to-end, and adversarial tests. Module-local tests stay in `modules/<module>/src/test/` and belong to the module owners.
     4	
     5	Territory (Phase 11 ruling 11): test-engineer writes under `tests/**` only. Ruling 11 withdrew ruling 4's grant of `modules/*/src/test/scala/**/adversarial/`, and `.claude/hooks/guard-paths.sh` enforces this. Every adversarial test therefore lives here.
     6	
     7	## Layout
     8	
     9	The sbt project `tests` (in `build.sbt`) compiles four source directories into its Test configuration. `tests/fixtures/` is its resource directory.
    10	
    11	| Directory | Package | Contents |
    12	|---|---|---|
    13	| `tests/adversarial/scholarchain/tests/support/` | `scholarchain.tests.support` | `StoreWorld`: a synthetic Phase 7 store-file world with injected text, an HTTP client, a capture log, and recorded model clients. `RawHttp`: raw loopback HTTP. `ScriptedGraph`: a graph port that blocks or throws. `GraphSpy`: records the graph-port calls the runtime makes and replays them through the live `NeptuneGraphReader` over a capturing client. `SparkWorld`: the one shared local SparkSession. |
    14	| `tests/adversarial/scholarchain/tests/adversarial/` | `scholarchain.tests.adversarial` | the adversarial suites |
    15	| `tests/contracts/scholarchain/tests/contracts/` | `scholarchain.tests.contracts` | configuration contract; the Spark and entity-resolution MatchPath contract |
    16	| `tests/integration/scholarchain/tests/integration/` | `scholarchain.tests.integration` | known-graph reads on the graph store; Spark known-graph analytics |
    17	| `tests/end-to-end/scholarchain/tests/endtoend/` | `scholarchain.tests.endtoend` | one bounded end-to-end fixture over the services |
    18	| `tests/fixtures/` | resources | `logback-test.xml`: library logging at WARN in the tests JVM. Logback reaches this classpath through services' Runtime scope. Without the file, logback logs Spark at DEBUG, which is about 120 MB per run. No project code logs through SLF4J. |
    19	
    20	The project sees the modules' compiled main code (`dependsOn(... % Test)`), not their test fixtures. Since repair-01 Part A it also has Spark SQL and the Hadoop client on its Test classpath, and it forks one JVM with the Spark JVM options and `-Xmx2g`. That JVM runs suites one at a time, with the repository root as working directory.
    21	
    22	## How to run
    23	
    24	Every sbt run sets `SPARK_LOCAL_IP=127.0.0.1`, with heap at most 6g.
    25	
    26	```text
    27	SPARK_LOCAL_IP=127.0.0.1 sbt -J-Xmx4g tests/test                       # all 151 tests (about 50 s)
    28	SPARK_LOCAL_IP=127.0.0.1 sbt -J-Xmx4g "tests/testOnly scholarchain.tests.adversarial.*"
    29	SPARK_LOCAL_IP=127.0.0.1 sbt -J-Xmx4g "tests/testOnly *SparkKnownGraphSpec"
    30	SPARK_LOCAL_IP=127.0.0.1 sbt -J-Xmx6g check                            # runs these with every module
    31	```
    32	
    33	As of work order 5: 151 tests, of which 141 pass and 10 are pending (the table below).
    34	
    35	No test calls AWS, Bedrock, or any network service. AWS SDK clients are JDK dynamic proxies of the client interfaces. Services, the fetch-counting server, and the Spark driver bind `127.0.0.1` only, with ephemeral ports. Every file goes to a JVM temporary directory that the test deletes, and Spark checkpoints go to one that is removed at JVM exit. No test is tagged `RequiresAws`.
    36	
    37	## Conventions (Phase 11 ruling 5)
    38	
    39	- **Determinism.**
    40	  - Concurrency is ordered with latches (`CountDownLatch`), so a held call stays held until the test releases it.
    41	  - Clocks are injected: `TickClock`, a frozen rate-limiter clock, a settable queue clock, and a settable DynamoDB `now`.
    42	  - Socket, client, and latch timeouts (5 to 30 s) are hang guards only. A healthy service answers in milliseconds, and the timeout fires only when the behaviour is broken.
    43	  - A latch that the code under test may count down after the response is awaited, never assumed (work order 4, finding 4).
    44	  - Spark fixtures use fixed graphs or `java.util.Random` with a fixed seed.
    45	- **One real-clock condition.** `WorkerRedeliveryAdversarialSpec`, "the composed query-api (Apps.queryApi) applies the rate limit to async requests too", uses the production limiter. `Apps.queryApi` injects no clock, so the test assumes three loopback requests take less than the limiter's 60 s window. Each request is already bounded by the 30 s client hang guard. The frozen-clock test next to it covers the limiter logic itself.
    46	- **Spark.**
    47	  - `SparkWorld.spark` is a lazily created `local[2]` session with UI off, 2 shuffle partitions, and the driver on 127.0.0.1. It is never stopped, because stopping it breaks later suites.
    48	  - Fixtures stay tiny: at most a few dozen nodes, which fits the shared `-Xmx2g`.
    49	  - Expected values come from hand-worked cases or from independent in-test references: union-find, min-degree peeling, synchronous label propagation, and per-node bridge sums.
    50	- **Hadoop.** Record readers run on local `file:` paths with a `Configuration`, `FileSplit`, and `TaskAttemptContextImpl`. Splits come from `DblpInputFormat.getSplits` itself. There is no mini-cluster.
    51	- **Synthetic data only.** Injection strings carry the marker `SYNTHETIC-INJECTION`, and secrets carry `SYNTHETICSECRET` or `SYNTHETIC-SECRET`. Researchers are labelled with classic computing names. No real personal data is used.
    52	- **Own logs.** Each services test passes its own `CaptureLog`, so it never reads or writes the services module's shared fixture log.
    53	- **Defect-exposing tests** (rulings 3 and 14) are wrapped in ScalaTest `pendingUntilFixed`.
    54	  - Each has a `// PENDING Dn (owner ...)` line.
    55	  - Without the wrapper the test fails. The evidence is in `scratch/phase-11-logs/wo3/pending-evidence.log` (D6 to D9), `scratch/phase-11-logs/rerun/pending-evidence.log` (D14 to D17), and `scratch/phase-11-logs/rerun-2/pending-evidence.log` (D18 to D20).
    56	  - Once the owner fixes the defect, the wrapper makes the test fail ("did not throw an exception"). The next test-engineer rerun then removes the wrapper, and the test must pass (ruling 14). Owners never edit `tests/**` (ruling 11).
    57

   105	| ID | Repair part | Test |
   106	|---|---|---|
   107	| D14 | Repair-02 Part A (rag-engineer, verifier-v8) | `UnsupportedClaimAdversarialSpec`: "a comparison or negation outside the verifier-v7 word lists (short of 10, lacks 10) is not verified" |
   108	
   109	### Wrappers removed in work order 4 (ruling 14)
   110	
   111	Repair-01 reports each of these fixed. Each test now passes without its wrapper.
   112	
   113	| ID | Repair part | Test |
   114	|---|---|---|
   115	| D1, D2, D3 | Part B (rag-engineer, verifier-v7) | `UnsupportedClaimAdversarialSpec`: quantity words; comparisons; negations |
   116	| D4 | Part B | `RagModelOutputAdversarialSpec`: "a throttled vector query (once, or every time) does not fail an answer the graph supports" |
   117	| D5 | Part C (service-engineer) | `ApiBodyAdversarialSpec`: "a declared body that never arrives does not hold the exchange open (refused, 401, or unread)" |
   118	| D10 | Part E (storage-engineer) | `WindowSummaryTamperAdversarialSpec`: "counts no coauthorship graph can have are refused ..." |
   119	| D11, D12 (two tests), D13 | Part D (domain-engineer) | `ConfigOmissionContractSpec` |
   120	
   121	## Human rulings 19 to 23 (after repair-01 and work order 4)
   122	
   123	- **Ruling 19.** D8 stays a residual risk with D6, D7, and D9. Its wrapper stays.
   124	- **Ruling 20.** D14 is confirmed major (rag-engineer). Repair-02 fixed it (verifier-v8), and work order 5 removed its wrapper; the test passes. Code review 2 covers repair-01, work order 4, repair-02, and work order 5.
   125	- **Ruling 21.** D15 and D16 (service-engineer) and D17 (hadoop-engineer) are minor residual risks for the Phase 12+ backlog. Their wrappers stay. The human's D17 measurement found no XML comment in any DBLP input, so D17 affects no existing output.
   126	- **Ruling 22.** The guard blocks of repair-01 and work order 4, and Part D's `du` over `scratch/`, are accepted deviations. No `du` or `find` runs over `scratch/` without pruning `scratch/stores-live/` and `scratch/er/adjudication-live/`.
   127	- **Ruling 23.** The justified wrappers at code review 2 are D6, D7, D8, D9, D15, D16, and D17. D18, D19, and D20 are new in work order 5, and code review 2 confirms or changes their severity and owner.
   128	
   129	## Not covered here, and why
   130	
   131	- **Live AWS** (ruling 2). There are no `RequiresAws` tests. The live adapters run against client proxies only. The DynamoDB proxy's semantics, a condition-expression subset with TTL by `:now`, are this suite's model of DynamoDB.
   132	- **Hadoop mini-cluster jobs and counters.** The record reader and input format are covered on local files. The J01 to J10 MapReduce drivers on a mini-cluster are not: they would need the Hadoop minicluster test artifacts, which are not on the classpath (build-engineer).
   133	- **The 30 s request read timeout itself.** The module's `RequestReadSpec` waits it out. Here, only "the timeout is set for every service" and the 1 s unread-body bound are tested, to keep check fast.
   134	- **Unreachable Spark inputs.** A mention map that sends one mention to two researchers cannot come from `Canonical.mentionMap`, which has one row per mention. `CoauthorGraph.build` has no check of its own for it.
   135	- **Verifier wordings repair-02 left uncovered on purpose.** RUNTIME.md section 21 lists them. Work order 5 measured them with a temporary probe, which was deleted afterwards (`scratch/phase-11-logs/rerun-2/probe-results.log`). The tests here do not assert them, so a later fix will not break a test. Against ada's stored 10, these are kept VERIFIED:
   136	  - "past 10", "is missing 10", "a minimum of 10", "a maximum of 10", "within 10", "toward 10", "pushing 10", "the better part of 10";
   137	  - "besides", "apart from", "other than", "nobody", "nothing";
   138	  - after-measure words ("10 publications and counting", "at minimum", "at most", "or more", ", roughly"), and comparison words separated from the number ("less than or equal to 10", "at the very least 10");
   139	  - "a mere 10".
   140	  Two of them fail through another rule: "none of" (0 is not a fact) and "exceeds the stored total of 10" (no measure name next to 10). Rules that RUNTIME.md section 12 documents are not asserted either: a negation after the number ("10 publications are absent from ..."), the ratio exception ("has publications above 10"), and a negation inside a measure-naming aside.

===== UNTRACKED FILE: tests/adversarial/scholarchain/tests/adversarial/ApiAuthAdversarialSpec.scala (140 lines) =====
     1	package scholarchain.tests.adversarial
     2	
     3	import org.scalatest.funsuite.AnyFunSuite
     4	import scholarchain.services.*
     5	import scholarchain.storage.{Item, KeyValueReadPort, TableName}
     6	import scholarchain.tests.support.StoreWorld.*
     7	
     8	import java.net.http.HttpRequest.BodyPublishers.ofString
     9	import java.util.concurrent.atomic.AtomicInteger
    10	
    11	/** Phase 11 WO3, ruling 1 target "API authentication bypass" and spec category "API authentication,
    12	  * authorization". query-api and graph-evidence-api on 127.0.0.1:0.
    13	  */
    14	class ApiAuthAdversarialSpec extends AnyFunSuite:
    15	
    16	  private def counting(w: World, reads: AtomicInteger) =
    17	    w.backend.copy(ports = w.backend.ports.copy(kv = new KeyValueReadPort:
    18	      def get(t: TableName, pk: String, sk: String): Option[Item] =
    19	        reads.incrementAndGet(); w.backend.ports.kv.get(t, pk, sk)
    20	      def query(t: TableName, pk: String, sk: String, limit: Int): Vector[Item] =
    21	        reads.incrementAndGet(); w.backend.ports.kv.query(t, pk, sk, limit)))
    22	
    23	  private def unauthorized(r: Reply, what: String): Unit =
    24	    assert(r.status == 401, s"$what: ${r.status} ${r.text}")
    25	    assert(r.error == "UNAUTHORIZED", what)
    26	    assert(r.body.obj.keySet == Set("error", "message", "traceId", "requestId", "retryable"), what)
    27	
    28	  test("path and method tricks never reach a protected query-api route without a valid key") {
    29	    withWorld() { w =>
    30	      val log = CaptureLog()
    31	      val reads = AtomicInteger()
    32	      w.query(log.log, counting(w, reads)) { c =>
    33	        val body = Some(java.net.http.HttpRequest.BodyPublishers.ofString(queryJson()))
    34	        val noKey = Seq("Content-Type" -> "application/json")
    35	        val paths = Vector(
    36	          "/v1/query",
    37	          "/v1/query/", // trailing slash
    38	          "/v1/%71uery", // percent-encoded 'q' decodes to the protected route
    39	          "/v1/./query",
    40	          "/healthz/../v1/query",
    41	          "/readyz/../v1/query"
    42	        )
    43	        val keyHeaders = Vector(
    44	          noKey,
    45	          noKey :+ ("X-Api-Key" -> ""),
    46	          noKey :+ ("X-Api-Key" -> ApiKey.toUpperCase),
    47	          noKey :+ ("X-Api-Key" -> ApiKey.take(ApiKey.length / 2)),
    48	          noKey :+ ("Authorization" -> s"Bearer $ApiKey"),
    49	          noKey :+ ("X-API-KEY-OVERRIDE" -> ApiKey),
    50	          noKey :+ ("X-Api-Key" -> s"$ApiKey,$ApiKey")
    51	        )
    52	        for p <- paths; h <- keyHeaders do unauthorized(c.send("POST", p, body, h), s"POST $p $h")
    53	        // GET routes, metrics, and lookalikes of the public routes.
    54	        val gets = Vector(
    55	          "/metrics",
    56	          "/metrics/",
    57	          "/healthz/../metrics",
    58	          "/healthz/x",
    59	          "/readyz/metrics",
    60	          s"/v1/jobs/job-${"0" * 32}",
    61	          s"/v1/jobs/job-${"0" * 32}/result",
    62	          s"/v1/explain/qry-${"0" * 32}",
    63	          "/v1/explain/%2E%2E%2Fmetrics"
    64	        )
    65	        gets.foreach(p => unauthorized(c.get(p, Seq()), s"GET $p"))
    66	        // The method override headers some frameworks honour do not reach POST without a key.
    67	        unauthorized(
    68	          c.send("GET", "/v1/query", None, Seq("X-HTTP-Method-Override" -> "POST")),
    69	          "override"
    70	        )
    71	        assert(reads.get == 0, "an unauthenticated request reached the key-value store")
    72	        // A valid key under a different header-name case is the same header (HTTP names are
    73	        // case-insensitive): it is accepted.
    74	        val lower = c.send("POST", "/v1/query", body, noKey :+ ("x-api-key" -> ApiKey))
    75	        assert(lower.status == 200, lower.text)
    76	      }
    77	      assert(!log.all.exists(_.contains(ApiKey)), "a log line holds the API key")
    78	    }
    79	  }
    80	
    81	  test("graph-evidence-api: no key, no graph or evidence read; encoded IDs do not change that") {
    82	    withWorld() { w =>
    83	      val log = CaptureLog()
    84	      w.graphEvidence(log.log) { c =>
    85	        Vector(
    86	          s"/v1/graph/nodes/${java.net.URLEncoder.encode(ada, "UTF-8")}",
    87	          s"/v1/graph/nodes/${java.net.URLEncoder.encode(ada, "UTF-8")}/neighbors?direction=both&depth=1&limit=1&edgeTypes=COAUTHORED_WITH",
    88	          s"/v1/evidence/ev-${"a" * 32}",
    89	          "/v1/graph/nodes/%2Fhealthz",
    90	          "/v1/evidence/..%2F..%2Fhealthz"
    91	        ).foreach(p => unauthorized(c.get(p, Seq()), p))
    92	        assert(c.get("/healthz", Seq()).status == 200)
    93	      }
    94	    }
    95	  }
    96	
    97	  test("unauthenticated requests do not consume the per-instance query rate budget") {
    98	    withWorld("services.query-api.query-rate-per-minute = 2") { w =>
    99	      val log = CaptureLog()
   100	      w.query(log.log) { c =>
   101	        val guess = Seq("Content-Type" -> "application/json", "X-Api-Key" -> "attacker-guess")
   102	        (1 to 25).foreach { _ =>
   103	          unauthorized(c.send("POST", "/v1/query", Some(ofString(queryJson())), guess), "flood")
   104	        }
   105	        // Both accepted queries of the minute are still available to the key holder.
   106	        assert(c.post("/v1/query", queryJson()).status == 200)
   107	        assert(c.post("/v1/query", queryJson()).status == 200)
   108	        assert(c.post("/v1/query", queryJson()).status == 429)
   109	      }
   110	    }
   111	  }
   112	
   113	  test("an unauthenticated oversized body is refused as 401, not read and not 400") {
   114	    withWorld() { w =>
   115	      val log = CaptureLog()
   116	      w.query(log.log) { c =>
   117	        val huge = queryJson("x" * (2 * w.cfg.services.maxBodyBytes))
   118	        val nope = Seq("Content-Type" -> "application/json", "X-Api-Key" -> "nope")
   119	        unauthorized(c.send("POST", "/v1/query", Some(ofString(huge)), nope), "huge body")
   120	      }
   121	    }
   122	  }
   123	
   124	  test("the API key comparison accepts exactly one value, of any length or content") {
   125	    val a = ApiKeyAuth(ApiKey)
   126	    assert(a.accepts(Some(ApiKey)))
   127	    Vector(
   128	      None,
   129	      Some(""),
   130	      Some(ApiKey + " "),
   131	      Some(" " + ApiKey),
   132	      Some(ApiKey + "\u0000"),
   133	      Some(ApiKey.dropRight(1)),
   134	      Some(ApiKey.reverse),
   135	      Some(ApiKey * 2),
   136	      Some("é" + ApiKey.drop(1))
   137	    ).foreach(h => assert(!a.accepts(h), h))
   138	    assert(scala.util.Try(ApiKeyAuth("")).isFailure, "an empty key must never be configured")
   139	    assert(!a.toString.contains(ApiKey))
   140	  }

    11	/** Phase 11 WO3, spec category "API malformed bodies", and ruling 1 target "injection through every
    12	  * text field" at the HTTP boundary. Every malformed input must give a typed 4xx error body, never
    13	  * 500, never a hang, and never an echo of the input.
    14	  */
    15	class ApiBodyAdversarialSpec extends AnyFunSuite:
    16	
    17	  private def clientError(r: Reply, what: String, codes: Set[String] = Set.empty): Unit =
    18	    assert(r.status >= 400 && r.status < 500, s"$what: ${r.status} ${r.text.take(300)}")
    19	    assert(
    20	      r.body.objOpt.exists(_.contains("traceId")),
    21	      s"$what: not the error model: ${r.text.take(200)}"
    22	    )
    23	    if codes.nonEmpty then assert(codes.contains(r.error), s"$what: ${r.error}")
    24	
    25	  test("deeply nested JSON within the size cap is a 400, not a stack overflow or a hang") {
    26	    withWorld() { w =>
    27	      val log = CaptureLog()
    28	      w.query(log.log) { c =>
    29	        val depth = (w.cfg.services.maxBodyBytes - 100) / 2
    30	        val arrays = s"""{"question": "q", "output": ${"[" * depth}${"]" * depth}}"""
    31	        assert(arrays.length < w.cfg.services.maxBodyBytes)
    32	        clientError(
    33	          c.post("/v1/query", arrays),
    34	          "nested arrays",
    35	          Set("INVALID_FIELD", "MALFORMED_JSON")
    36	        )
    37	        val objects =
    38	          s"""{"question": "q", "constraints": ${"""{"a":""" * (depth / 3)}1${"}" * (depth / 3)}}"""
    39	        assert(objects.length < w.cfg.services.maxBodyBytes)
    40	        clientError(
    41	          c.post("/v1/query", objects),
    42	          "nested objects",
    43	          Set("INVALID_FIELD", "MALFORMED_JSON", "UNSUPPORTED_CONSTRAINT")
    44	        )
    45	        // The service is still healthy afterwards.
    46	        assert(c.post("/v1/query", queryJson()).status == 200)
    47	      }
    48	    }
    49	  }

   171	  private def malformed(b: String) =
   172	    b.endsWith("trailing") || b == "{\"question\": \"q\"" || b.startsWith("\uFEFF")

   212	          assert(r.status == 422, s"$b: ${r.status} ${r.text}")
   213	          assert(!r.text.contains("OR 1=1"), "the error echoes the request")
